@@4444786 <h> A guide to the Durban climate change chronicles <p> A worker pulls a trash bin as he and others clean up the beach area in Durban , South Africa , on Tuesday . ( SCHALK van ZUYDAM / The Associated Press ) <p> For a species supposedly facing an existential threat , mankind seems remarkably willing to endlessly argue over issues of considerably less significance -- if you believe the danger is real . <p> I 'm talking of global warming/climate change . <p> And so , as the latest round of global climate change talks opened Monday in warm , rainy , seaside Durban , South Africa , the world 's media were busily reporting on the yawning gaps between the positions of the planet 's biggest emitters of greenhouse gases . <p> China and dozens of other developing or emerging nations -- which were all exempt from the legally binding emission targets of the 1997 Kyoto Accord -- want industrialized countries to agree to be bound by those provisions beyond 2012 . But developed countries like Canada , Japan and the United States say the @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ which all GHG-emitting nations , such as China , India and Brazil , are to be included . <p> The chances a deal will be struck in Durban are remote , observers say . <p> If humanity 's future existence is supposedly in play here , one wonders why everyone ca n't agree , for the good of everyone . <p> Three possible reasons . <p> *Few of the world 's prime power brokers believe the consequences of a warming climate will be as apocalyptic as doomsayers have breathlessly predicted . <p> Some may doubt the reliability of long-term rising temperature forecasts altogether . Others may agree that global warming will bring challenges , but see them as largely overblown . <p> In this scenario , the key for political leaders is ensuring -- while making politically correct noises about how " concerned " they are about climate -- that they do n't hobble their nations ' economies with restraints while their global competitors remain unencumbered . <p> *Key global political decision-makers , though convinced of the magnitude of the danger posed by climate change , assume they have some " @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ . <p> They either believe mankind has more time in which to act to forestall the direst outcomes or that technological innovations will surely emerge in time to help stop or mitigate any climate disaster . <p> Again , though , the end result is almost the same . Political leaders , while sincere about their concern , feel they must balance what is ultimately needed with protecting their own nations ' economic competitiveness now . <p> *In the last possibility , the world 's leaders believe a climate catastrophe is imminent . <p> Despite that , however , the domestic political pressure to oppose any deal perceived as undercutting their own state 's national interest is so intense that it creates deadlock . That 's because each country 's leaders define " defending their interests " differently . <p> The outcome , you 'll note , is familiar , though for different reasons . Though not in their collective self-interest , political leaders play " chicken " with their global counterparts , fighting over who does what , accomplishing nothing , despite the obvious consequences . <p> Some observers have @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ to what actually happened on the tiny island in the eastern Pacific well known for its giant rock-head statues . Despite inhabitants relying on the island 's trees for sustenance , over the centuries the islanders ' tribal rivalries -- focused on the perceived prestige associated with the giant heads , which required logs to roll them into place -- led the inhabitants to cut down every last tree . <p> Thus ended the Easter Island civilization . <p> To further complicate matters , you 'd also have to assume that all three viewpoints as explained above are very likely in play at any international talks about climate change . <p> The bottom line , however , remains that , regardless of the reasons , there 's virtually no chance the Kyoto Protocol , which only called for binding emission cuts from some nations , will be extended . <p> That 's the political reality . <p> What no one really knows , for certain , is what the scientific reality will be . <h> Comments(10) <p> Kyoto was dead when Clinton/Gore would n't even send it to the US senate @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ India produced . @@4446619 <h> U.S. , Saudi Arabia stall work on US$100B UN climate fund <p> Work stalled on the Green Climate Fund as the U.S. , Saudi Arabia and Venezuela raised concerns about the structure of the United Nations mechanism that would channel up to US$100-billion a year in aid to developing nations . <p> Maite Nkoana-Mashabane , the South African minister overseeing the annual round of climate talks , said she 'd undertake " informal " consultations about how to overcome the objections to technical details about how the fund will work . <p> The decision is a setback for the UN , which is working to include the fund in a package of measures for fighting global warming being discussed at talks in Durban this week and next . Mashabane may be forced to re-open a text detailing the fund for debate at the talks , raising the risk work on it wo n't be done when the meeting finishes on Dec. 9 . <p> " You 'll get a domino effect as everyone asks for changes , " Stefan Krug , climate finance spokesperson for Greenpeace , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ , may jeopardize the fund . <p> The U.S. , Saudi Arabia and Latin American nations in the Alba negotiating bloc say they ca n't accept a draft plan for a green climate fund . <p> The draft raises " substantive concerns " and contains " errors and inconsistencies , " said Jonathan Pershing , a State Department envoy leading the U.S. delegations . He did n't elaborate his concerns . The Venezuelan envoy , speaking on behalf of Alba , said the plan would hurt developing countries ' access to resources . <p> Saudis Want Compensation <p> Saudi Arabia , the world 's largest crude exporter , is seeking compensation from the United Nations ' Green Climate Fund as climate rules may lead to a loss in oil income , the kingdom 's envoy to UN talks in South Africa said . <p> " Those developing countries who are going to be adversely impacted as a result of climate policies should be assisted by the Fund to adapt to such impacts including helping us in achieving economic diversification and reduce our dependency on the exportation of crude oil , " @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ today in an e-mailed response to questions . <p> The Green Climate Fund , central to agreements reached last year by UN treaty negotiators in Cancun , Mexico , is being discussed at talks in Durban , which began this week . The world 's richest countries pledged to channel $100 billion annually by 2020 , part of it through the fund , to help poorer nations reduce greenhouse-gas emissions from energy production and adapt to effects of global warming such as rising sea levels . <p> Saudi Arabia " will never contribute " to the Fund , which should be financed by the governments of developed nations , al- Sabban said . <p> Developed Countries ' Responsibility <p> " It is not acceptable to ask developing countries to contribute to the fund , because and as stated in the Climate Convention , it is the responsibility of the developed countries , " he wrote , referring to the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change or UNFCCC . @@4446696 <h> End Floods -- Fill in Your Basement <p> After an astonishingly horrible 15 basement floodings in eight months , East Windsor homeowner Naomi Gatrall is ready to throw in the towel . <p> " I wish ... I could fill this basement in , " she told me yesterday as close to 90 mm of rain was falling outside and disgusting little creepy-crawlies were being forced into her basement along with , for the umpteenth time , stinking municipal sewage . Read my story in today 's Star . <p> Do n't blame Naomi . A home inspection professional gave the all-clear before she bought the place three years ago . She has a backup pump to cover the backup pump for the sump pump , and , on city advice , recently replaced her working and fairly new backflow prevention valve with a brand new one . She 's had weeping tile installed along both the inside and outside of her basement wall . <p> Yesterday saw a record-breaking rainfall ( up to 90 mm in the city ) in a month that is already tops @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ was already the rainiest on record . Dig below the surface anywhere in Windsor or Essex County and it 's like one of those squishy sponge toys that 's been dunked in the bathtub -- our soils are sopping wet . <p> When thousands of homeowners are being urged by municipal authorities to stop flushing their toilets or else risk flooding their neighbours ' basements , you know we 've reached a turning point . <p> Naomi 's dark and only half-joking suggestion of filling in that basement ? Maybe not such a crazy idea , when you talk to municipal officials . <p> Below-ground basements were originally never intended as today 's living and entertainment spaces . It 's where you stored the canned preserves and turnips for the winter and stored all that junk you no longer needed , but maybe someday might . <p> Mike Palanacki , the city 's executive director of operations , told me he has about $20 million to juggle annually among the overwhelming needs and demands of an ancient sewage system where there 's an " infrastructur deficit " ( or backlog in @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ I think the flooding situation is never going to be solved , " said Mike , one of the city 's more refreshingly candid bosses . In an age of climate change-induced severe storm occurrences , triggering super-saturated soils ( think living on top of a wetland ) , he suggests Windsorites may want to reconsider the idea of even having basements . <p> And if 2011 is the new benchmark , it might make sense .
